Nothing in-corpus tests fungibility — CRWV discloses no utilisation or revenue split by workload | **⚠ nuance / UNVERIFIED** — management's claim, logged as a claim; it removes the bear's mechanism only if true |
| **🆕 Funding cost by counterparty** (row added 2026-09-21 from the Redburn 148pp PRIMARY) | **Intrator, 09-08 (MANAGEMENT):** CoreWeave borrows *"at par or close to at par with many of the hyperscalers"*; **Robbins, 09-21 (MANAGEMENT):** 2026 capex funding described as complete at the parent level | Redburn's full DDTL ladder prices that claim precisely: the **Meta**-backed DDTL 4.0 at **SOFR + 225bp** (≈5.9% all-in) IS *"only modestly above the cost of debt for investment-grade hyperscalers such as Amazon and Alphabet"*. But the Aug-26 DDTL 5.5 to an **undisclosed** counterparty prices at **SOFR + 550bp** (≈9% all-in) — **325bp wider on the newest paper**, on a 5yr facility standing against ~3yr customer contracts | **⚠ nuance, and it cuts both ways** — the CEO's claim is TRUE where a hyperscaler signs the offtake and FALSE where one does not. The spread is a function of the COUNTERPARTY, not of CoreWeave; the bear case is therefore about the MIX of future offtakers, which is unforecastable from disclosure |
| **🆕 Enterprise breadth — the diversification leg** (row added 2026-09-22 from the UBS Evidence Lab initiation) | **Intrator / release, 08-11 (MANAGEMENT):** *"customer demand is accelerating, as **enterprise adoption broadens**"*, with Cognition, Databricks, HRT, Periodic Labs, Rescale, Runway ML expanded and Bentley, Caterpillar, Grammarly, Isomorphic Labs, Sunday Robotics named; restated 09-14 as *"~75% of customers use at least THREE CoreWeave services"* | **UBS Evidence Lab, 6th semi-annual US AI Business Survey (~140 organisations), 2026-09-22:** neoclouds (**CoreWeave, Lambda**) are the preferred AI-infrastructure provider for **<10% of respondents and show *"no material improvement from prior surveys"***, while **Microsoft slightly EXTENDED its #1 lead** (AWS #2, Google Cloud #3). In the same note, UBS's own customer checks include a **churn** — *"we quite recently ENDED the CoreWeave relationship and shifted those workloads to our own data centers… we are one of the largest banks in the world"* — and UBS's own gloss on the F500 list: *"here is where we and investors would like to see more progress"* | **✗ contests, and this is the cleanest contradiction on the page** — the attach rate measures DEPTH inside a narrow base and is not in dispute; the survey measures BREADTH, and breadth has not moved in six months of semi-annual reads. Note the direction of the disagreement: UBS is a **BUYER of the stock** publishing the datapoint that most undercuts the enterprise leg, which is why it should be weighted rather than discounted |

**Q3'26 synthesis (opened 2026-08-11):** the debate has moved. For four quarters this page's central question was *"can margins inflect before the debt does?"* — the Q2 print answers **yes, earlier than either bulls or bears modelled** (adj. OM ~5% vs ~3% bogey, EBITDA 59%). Two questions replace it: (1) **does it compound** — JPM's 7.0% vs 7.5% F3Q consensus is the near-term scoreboard; and (2) **does the funding cost fall** now that [[NVDA]]'s $500bn third-party financing platform exists and, per @firstadopter, is aimed at neoclouds rather than hyperscalers. The unglamorous risk is unchanged: **$9.4B of quarterly capex against $679M of CFO**, i.e. the thesis is still financed, not funded. **🆕 Updated 2026-08-12 once the primary transcript was read: question (2) is now half-answered by the company itself, and question (1) has to be restated. On funding cost, management disclosed a ~300bp reduction in weighted-average cost of debt over the past year ≈ $1.1B of annualized interest saving — the first hard number on the variable both bulls and bears agree is binding. On margin, the inflection is SEQUENTIAL ONLY: adjusted operating income rose from $21M to $128M q/q but FELL from $200M y/y, while interest expense went $267M → $640M and is guided to $860–940M in Q3. None of the four same-day broker notes carried the interest line. So the honest scoreboard for Q3 is not "7.0% vs 7.5% operating margin" alone — it is whether PF operating income of $200–260M closes any of the gap to $860–940M of interest. Third question added: how big is the roll-off book? Management declined to size it ("a very limited part of our fleet"), so the recontracting-ASP debate that the A100-to-2029 quote is being used to settle remains unquantified.** **🆕 UPDATED 2026-08-20 — question (2) now has an EXTERNAL answer, and it is split. On CRWV's own paper the answer is YES, the funding cost is falling: MS Credit has the CRWV term loan (S+550, BB, 9.15%) tightening 584→527, the largest move in its leveraged-loan table, and CRWV now issuing EUR high yield ($2bn) — independent corroboration of the ~300bp WACD compression management claimed. But the same note prices CRWV OFFTAKE 300–600bp behind [[AMZN]] offtake on physically comparable data-center projects (APLD 8.2% / CORZ 11.5% / ELKGVP 12.0% / EDGCOM 14.2% vs STNGRY 7.9–8.8%) — so the borrower is getting cheaper while the counterparty is not, and the second curve is the one Bernstein's structural bear lives on. THAT SPREAD IS THE NEW BEST SCOREBOARD ON THIS PAGE: it is observable, dated, and it resolves an argument that four quarters of equity-side opinion has not. A FOURTH question is now added, and it is the one the page has no answer to: what happens to CRWV's scarcity value as the neocloud field goes from two credible names to *"hundreds"* (JPM Asia, 08-20) / 20 buying optical networks (Wolfe, 08-14)? Falling customer concentration and falling operator scarcity are the same event seen from two sides. And one uncomfortable supplier-side observation to carry into it: receivables days rose materially at Super Micro when CoreWeave and xAI were larger shares of its sales (Wolfe, 08-14) — CRWV takes prepays from customers and stretches suppliers, which is good treasury and a poor counterparty profile at once.** **🆕🔴 Updated 2026-08-21 — A THIRD QUESTION IS NOW ON THE TABLE, AND IT INVERTS THE LEVERAGE DEBATE: (3) *is the leverage the bear case or the bull case?* Arete answers "bull", and does it with the same balance sheet the bears cite — because CRWV's DDTL contract-financing stack makes its EQUITY value smaller than Nebius's against slightly greater near-term unsold GW, CRWV is the SECOND-most levered listed neocloud to rising compute pricing (behind `IREN`, ahead of [[NBIS]]), with unsold MW per $bn of market cap of 50 at YE28E vs Nebius's 33.** That is the first framing on this page in which "financed, not funded" is an argument FOR owning the equity rather than against it, and it arrives with the highest PT the page has carried (**BUY, $317, raised from $303, revenue 26%/37% above consensus in '28E/'29E**). ⚠️ **It does not retire question (1) or (2) — it re-prices them: the same fixed-debt structure that amplifies upside on repricing is what the credit market is charging 300–600bp for on the offtake side, and Arete's own timing caveat is that the new pricing reaches CRWV mainly through GPU contracts maturing from 4Q26 onward, not this quarter** (Arete Research "NEO Clouds: Greater Pricing Power", 2026-08-21). **🆕 09-06 update — CoreWeave is the named first recipient of production Vera Rubin.** [[DELL]]'s CEO, first-party: ***"We have delivered the world's first production NVIDIA Vera Rubin NVL72 racks to CoreWeave"*** (@MichaelDell, relayed by @dnystedt, 2026-09-06). This is an **allocation** datapoint sourced from the OEM rather than from CRWV's IR, and it closes out the ramp-risk flag this page carried from SemiAnalysis in the Q1'26 window (Vera Rubin racks *"still 'Eng/Quality Samples'"*). ⚠️ **It is bounded to exactly that: no rack count, no MW, no dollar value, no revenue-service date, and therefore no read across to the ~4.2 GW contracted mark, the $104bn backlog or the adj.-capex line. The live test is whether first-in-line on the new platform converts into the pricing this page is tracking (~$16M/MW/yr on long-term contracts) at the Q3'26 print, or is simply a delivery-sequence courtesy.**

**🆕 Updated 2026-09-22 (UBS initiation, Keirstead/Giuditta — Buy, PT $120):** the synthesis needs one structural change and one scoreboard change. **STRUCTURAL:** the three-way pricing fork this page opened on 09-18/09-21 (Nscale's filed mid-teens CEILING vs CoreWeave management's mid-teens FLOOR vs Redburn's "a real price paid by the wrong counterparties") gains a fourth number that is **not a fourth opinion** — UBS's **~$11bn/GW is the BLENDED REALISED rate across the whole installed book**, a backward-looking average that is arithmetically compatible with every side of the fork. The correct reading of the page is therefore a **TIER ladder, not a disagreement**: legacy/installed ~$11bn → hyperscaler-and-lab 5-6yr take-or-pay ~$12-16bn → merchant 1-3yr term ~$20-25bn → 3-6 month short-dated ~$40bn per GW annualised, with UBS now supplying the term and basis for the top rung. Model the mix, not the price. **SCOREBOARD:** the falsifiable near-term line has moved from JPM's 7.0%-vs-7.5% Q3 adj.-OM print to **UBS's implied 4Q26 non-GAAP operating-margin guide of 14%** — because that is the number that has to print for the 5% → 25-30% margin path (and therefore the entire $120 base case, which is built on CY28 MARGIN and MULTIPLE, not on revenue) to stay credible. **AND THE ONE THING BOTH THE BULL AND THE BEAR NOTES OF THIS WEEK AGREE ON: the enterprise-breadth leg is not working yet.** Redburn (Sell, 09-21) questions whether the pipeline converts into attractive returns; UBS (Buy, 09-22) prints a ~140-organisation survey showing neoclouds stuck below 10% of preferred-provider mentions with no improvement, plus a global-bank customer that just left for on-prem. Two houses on opposite sides of the stock, same finding on the same leg — that is the highest-conviction negative on this page right now, and it is independent of the credit debate.

If we assume this is for one of those then the price equates to **$16M/MW/year over the term.** Note that Anthropic also signed a similar **6 year deal with Nscale for $45B** in a Virginia data center giving it access to **460MW** of IT capacity and **that equates to a similar $16M/MW/year.** On a smaller level, **Bitdeer (BTDR) contracted 9MW of compute in Asia for 5 years for $800M** which was at a **similar $16M/MW/year.**"* 🔴🔴 **THE CONCLUSION, VERBATIM, AND IT IS EXPLICITLY A READ-THROUGH TO THIS PAGE: *"As a follow through on our theme to be **long compute**, the following deals continue to signal **HIGHER REVENUE PER MW for CRWV, [[SPCX]] and [[NBIS]]**… **A HIGHER FLOOR IS BEING SET FOR LONG TERM DEALS. $16M/MW/year is 60% HIGHER than the $10M/MW/year long term deals in the past year. The market has consistently debated the LIFESPAN OF GPUs and a term deal for 6 years is a move in the right direction.**"*** ➤ **A creditworthy hyperscale-class tenant signing SIX-YEAR terms is the single best available third-party answer to the "GPUs are 3-4 year assets" bear that caps every neocloud multiple on this wiki. It does not prove the useful life; it proves what a sophisticated counterparty will contract for.** ⚠️⚠️ **UNIT GUARD, MANDATORY AND NON-NEGOTIABLE ON THIS PAGE: $16M/MW/year is **CONTRACT REVENUE to the compute provider, per MW, per YEAR.** It is NOT capex, NOT build cost, and must NEVER be netted against a $/GW construction anchor or chained into a build-cost series. ⚠️ **AND THE 22V FIGURE RESTS ON AN EXPLICIT ASSUMPTION ("if we assume this is for one of those" leases) — corroborated but not confirmed: BofA independently sizes the Lambda deal at *"6 year, 350 MW"*, and @wallstengine reports the **campus is 700MW under 15-year leases that Hut 8 said *"could represent a ~$20B facility"***. So the $35bn maps to roughly half the campus and the per-MW maths holds — but the lease allocation is inferred, not disclosed.** |

Execution has been proven, but the path to shareholder returns is still heavy."*** ➜ **His Q2'26 decomposition, all from the print: revenue **$2.575bn**, adj EBITDA **$1.510bn (59%)**, but **adjusted OPERATING income only $128M** against **net interest expense of $640M** and a **net loss of $626M**; PP&E purchases incl. capitalised internal-use software **$6.422bn**; at end-June **principal borrowings $35.55bn** and **operating lease liabilities $16.32bn**. **RPO $103.7bn** against top-three customers at **36% / 26% / 10%** of Q2 revenue. *"However many contracts there are, what belongs to shareholders is the cash that survives interest, lease costs and customer concentration risk."*** 🔴 **THE VALUATION BRIDGE, WHICH IS THE SHARPEST FRAMING OF THE RPO NUMBER ON THIS PAGE: market cap **~$47.8bn**, EV reflecting total debt and cash **~$77.9bn**, and **the capital claim INCLUDING OPERATING LEASES ~$94.2bn — roughly 91% of the $103.7bn RPO.** *"RPO is REVENUE, NOT PROFIT. Delays in bringing capacity online or margin compression can show up sharply in shareholder value."*** ✅ **THE BULL SIDE HE GRANTS, AND IT IS THE OPERATIONAL ONE: CRWV *"has demonstrated the most about turning secured power into actual revenue"* — **~500MW of active power added in Q2 to reach 1.5GW total against 3.7GW contracted**, plus the industry's **first bring-up and validation of Vera Rubin NVL72**. His mechanism for why that compounds: *"the inspection procedures and failure data accumulated in the first Rubin cluster can SHORTEN TIME TO SERVICE in the next batch,"* an asset that *"does not appear on the balance sheet, nor can a later entrant simply buy it with money."*** 🔴 **HIS ENTRY CONDITION, WHICH IS A CLEAN FALSIFIABLE TEST THE PAGE CAN TRACK: wait until **active power grows FASTER THAN PLAN** and **cash flow after net interest and lease costs improves for AT LEAST TWO CONSECUTIVE QUARTERS**.** ⚠️⚠️ **AND THE NVDA-SUPPORT AUDIT, WHICH DIRECTLY QUALIFIES THE FINANCING NARRATIVE THIS PAGE CARRIES: against *"the market has taken this as a sign that the funding problem for neoclouds has been SOLVED,"* he goes to the 10-Q — NVDA's **maximum guarantee exposure $108.5bn**, of which **$105bn is the ~4.25GW SB Energy Ohio project tied to an OpenAI affiliate and is UNRELATED to the neoclouds**; **AI-cloud-related guarantees total just $3.5bn**, and even that is disclosed *"as an AGGREGATE guarantee for SELECT AI cloud partners — public disclosure alone does NOT establish that it is allocated to CoreWeave, Nebius and IREN."* Separately the **$36bn capacity purchase commitment** (typically over six years) *"is NOT fixed revenue"* because *"if operators sell that capacity to other customers, NVIDIA's purchase obligation FALLS ACCORDINGLY."* And the **$500bn+ Apollo/BlackRock/Blackstone/Brookfield/Goldman/KKR compute-financing platform (10-Aug)** is *"a MEMORANDUM OF UNDERSTANDING with large financial institutions rather than COMMITTED CAPITAL, and individual projects still have to be underwritten separately."*** ➜ **This is the most disciplined parsing of the NVDA backstop on this page and it cuts against the reflexive read of the $36bn/$500bn headlines. | **08-25** | 🔴 **SemiAnalysis · Dylan Patel podcast w/ Daniel Grozdea** (annual long-form; ⚠️ **one practitioner’s forward estimates delivered on a podcast — not company guidance, not consensus, no rating or PT**) | capital / preco | **mixed** | **READ-THROUGH — no CRWV mark, but the clearest statement on this page of the POWER STRUCTURE a contract-first neocloud operates inside, and it is not flattering.** 🔴 **Verbatim: “For compute that you can build without financing… If Meta can build compute, Microsoft, Amazon, SpaceX can build compute WITHOUT FINDING A CUSTOMER, just saying, fuck it, I’m gonna build this compute, and then turn around and wait till it’s already built, THEY NOW CONTROL WHAT’S GOING ON. So most compute is contracted well before it’s built… Other people, you go to a random cloud, they’re like, okay, I’m going to build a gigawatt of compute… I need to turn around and FIND A CUSTOMER. If I want to find a customer, I need to find the CAPITAL. Who’s going to give me the capital on the customer? THE CUSTOMER HAS TO SIGN A DEAL. And then I take the customer’s commitment to the credit markets and I raise the capital.”** ➜ **That is precisely CoreWeave’s model described from the outside, and it names the structural asymmetry: balance-sheet builders capture the option value of unsold capacity in a rising market; contract-first builders sold that option to the counterparty when they signed. It is the same asymmetry the [[META]] and SpaceX “hoarding” row on the META page describes from the other side.** 🔴 **THE PRICE CALL THAT MATTERS FOR THE 2027 RATE DEBATE: asked what compute sold at 40 (US$bn/GW) transacts for at the end of next year, Patel says “I think MOST COMPUTE WILL STILL CONTINUE TO TRANSACT AT SUB $20 BILLION A GIGAWATT” — even at end-2027 — “BECAUSE ALL OF IT HAS TO BE FINANCED.” Financing, not demand, is what caps the realised price for the contract-first cohort. Against that, he expects the LABS to have to pay “25, 30, 50 million dollars a megawatt” to take 70-80% of incremental compute in 2028.** ⚠️⚠️ **UNIT DISCIPLINE: every $/GW and $/MW figure here is REVENUE per unit of capacity per year, not build cost, not capex and not debt. Do not net them against this page’s capex or debt-per-MW marks, and do not chain them into a GW series.** (SemiAnalysis · Dylan Patel podcast w/ Daniel Grozdea, 2026-08-25) |

| **08-16** | **The Information · Catherine Perloff — "Nebius and CoreWeave Tout Short-Term Cloud Deals, While AWS Goes Long"** | **guidance** | **mixed** | **★ THE CONTRACT-DURATION DEBATE, NAMED AS A DELIBERATE PITCH RATHER THAN AN ACCIDENT: "This week, [[NBIS]] and CoreWeave BOTH PITCHED INVESTORS ON A NEW BUSINESS OPPORTUNITY — SHORTER-TERM CONTRACTS," on the argument that these "would allow them to CASH IN ON SURGING PRICES FOR AI COMPUTE CAPACITY AMID A CRUNCH FOR GPUs" — and "investors seemed to agree, with SHARES IN BOTH COMPANIES MOVING SHARPLY HIGHER."** **The counter-model, from the largest player: [[AMZN]] "used its earnings call to highlight that it was selling MOST of its AI capacity via FIVE-YEAR DEALS," with Jassy arguing the long duration is what "ensure[s] AWS will generate enough revenue to MAKE MONEY ON ITS DATA CENTER BUILD-OUT" — and "Amazon's stock is UP OVER 11% from its July 30 call."** ⚠️ **The market rewarded BOTH narratives in the same week, which should be the flag rather than the comfort. The two positions are not reconcilable on pricing: short duration only beats long duration if spot compute prices stay elevated or rise, which is the opposite of what a long-contract seller is underwriting. For a page whose central bear is contract quality vs depreciation schedule, this is the debate in one line — and it moves CRWV's risk from COUNTERPARTY toward PRICE.** ⚠️ **Single-outlet trade press; no contract sizes, durations, prices or mix disclosed.** |

At a minimum, this should support A REITERATION OF FULL-YEAR REVENUE AND ACTIVE POWER TARGETS as large new capacity blocks appear to be tracking ON, OR IN SOME CASES SLIGHTLY AHEAD OF, SCHEDULE."** ⚠️ **A 250–350MW q/q addition versus ~150MW prior is a specific, falsifiable number and the single cleanest thing to check on 08-11.** ⚠️ **THE MARGIN MECHANIC, and it is a genuine near-term negative stated by the bull: "The FLIP-SIDE TO RAPID CAPACITY GROWTH IS NEAR-TERM MARGIN PRESSURE FROM COSTS STARTING TO COME ONLINE AHEAD OF REVENUE (e.g. LEASES, POWER AND DEPRECIATION); we believe this LIMITS OPPORTUNITY FOR UPSIDE TO NON-GAAP OPERATING MARGINS THAT WE NOW MODEL REMAINING IN THE LSD% – MSD% RANGE THROUGH C3Q BEFORE STARTING TO INFLECT IN C4Q."** So: low-to-mid single-digit non-GAAP operating margins for two more quarters, inflecting only in Q4 — anyone owning this into the print should not expect margin relief. **On capex, and note the definitional point matters: "We DON'T ANTICIPATE A FURTHER CHANGE TO FULL-YEAR CAPEX GUIDANCE, absent some acceleration/pull-forward of capacity, after last quarter's increase ATTRIBUTED TO COMPONENT COSTS, and REMIND INVESTORS THE COMPANY DEFINES CAPEX BASED ON WHEN ASSETS ARE PLACED INTO REVENUE SERVICE, NOT ON CASH OUTLAYS."** ⚠️ **That definition is a trap for cross-company capex comparisons — CoreWeave's capex line is not on the same basis as a hyperscaler's cash capex.** 🔴 **THE DIRECT REBUTTAL TO THE OVERBUILD BEAR THIS WINDOW CREATED: "Any concern about [[SPCX]] AND [[META]] SELLING 'EXCESS CAPACITY' EXTERNALLY SIGNALING RISK OF AN OVERBUILD SEEMS MISPLACED"** — worth holding against [[ORCL]]'s own bear list from UBS the same week, which names SPCX, META and OpenAI-via-Pike-County as competitors selling/consuming compute. **Two houses, same facts, opposite conclusions; both now on the wiki.** Zelnick's four bull pillars into the back half and the late-September user conference start with "SUPPORTIVE CONTRACTING ENVIRONMENT" and centre on "underlying ROIC and the DIFFERENTIATION AND BREADTH of CoreWeave's portfolio" becoming apparent, on shares ">30% OFF MAY HIGHS." **Positioning note: Roundhill launched a NEOCLOUD ETF on 2026-08-06 with CRWV at 27.30% (second weight behind [[NBIS]] at 30.83%; IREN 7.72%, HUT 6.32%, WULF 4.97%) — a new marginal, price-insensitive holder of the equity (@ParadisLabs, 2026-08-06).** (Deutsche Bank · Brad Zelnick / Bhavin Shah / Daniel Knauff / Yash Kejriwal, 2026-08-06) |
| 07-22 | **★ AlphaSense expert call — Former Senior Director of Product (hyperscaler + neo-cloud GTM/deal-structuring; names CoreWeave as first-hand experience) — "Hyperscalers Hold Strategic Advantages in AI Infrastructure Investments"** | valuation | **bear** | ⚠️ **Dating and provenance first: PUBLISHED 2026-07-22 but INTERVIEWED 2026-04-23 — a three-month-old view, filed by AlphaSense under primary company GOOGL. It is routed here because CoreWeave, not Google, is the concrete subject: the expert speaks from direct CoreWeave experience ("Even at CoreWeave when I was we started it…") and the note is structurally a hyperscaler-vs-neocloud comparison.** 🔴 **THE PAYBACK SPREAD, which is the whole bear case in one number: _"When I say hyperscalers I'm including AWS, Microsoft, and Google… If I look at somebody like a hyperscaler, you are looking at a THREE TO FIVE payback on GPU. The infrastructure build is attractive for them because they can MONETIZE THE SAME ASSET ACROSS MULTIPLE REVENUE STREAMS. They can accept longer direct paybacks, because the ECOSYSTEM ROI IS BROADER. When you look at NEOCLOUDS, we look at TWO TO THREE YEARS PAYBACK PERIOD."_** And the explicit rule of thumb: _"if you're looking at an agile hyperscaler, STRONG PAYBACK IS LESS THAN THREE YEARS. Acceptable would be 3-5, RISKY WOULD BE MORE THAN FIVE, unless it's very strategic in nature. When I look at a new cloud, I would want to pay back in TWO TO THREE YEARS MAX ACCEPTABLE. THE RISK IS GREATER THAN THREE AND A HALF OR FOUR YEARS."_ ⚠️ **The asymmetry is the point and it is structural, not cyclical: the same asset, bought at the same price, must pay back roughly TWICE AS FAST at a neocloud because there is only one revenue stream against it — and the expert names the aggravating factors as _"more sensitive to DEBT COST, or even CUSTOMER CONCENTRATION, or GPU RESIDUAL… they need more CASH CONVERSION to support the growth and the obligation."_ That is the [[CRWV]] bear case stated by a practitioner rather than a short-seller, and it maps onto every leg this page already tracks (leverage, [[MSFT]]/[[META]] concentration, residual-value risk on contracted fleets).** **The mitigant the expert volunteers, and it is CoreWeave-specific: _"training clusters. Can they justify longer payback if backed… if I committed multi-contracts or customers like META DID WITH COREWEAVE?"_ — i.e. a large committed anchor contract is the recognised mechanism for stretching an acceptable neocloud payback, which is precisely what this page's Meta and OpenAI backlog items are.** 🔴 **SECOND ITEM — THE SCARCITY PREMIUM IS EXPECTED TO COMPRESS, and this is the forward call: the expert _"expects the market to shift toward MORE COMMITTED, LONG-TERM CONTRACTS for GPU usage as AI workloads mature and SUPPLY INCREASES, REDUCING THE SCARCITY PREMIUM seen in earlier cycles,"_ with the revenue mix _"shifting from predominantly HOURLY/SPOT RENTALS during periods of GPU scarcity to a higher proportion of COMMITTED, LONG-TERM CONTRACTS."_ Contract terms cited: one-, three- and five-year deals, _"longer-term contracts offer greater discounts."_** ⚠️ **Two-sided for this page rather than simply bearish: committed contracts are exactly what lengthens acceptable payback and de-risks the model, but the mechanism getting there is PRICE. Set it against the Gavin Baker datapoint on [[NVDA]] that B200 rentals ROSE 50-60% in six-to-seven months, and against the ~20-25%-over-18-months post-generation decay rate from the AWS expert file on [[AMZN]] — this call is a three-month-old forecast of compression, not an observation of it.** **Third: the KPI set the expert says is actually run — _"GPU utilization… realized price per GPU hour, then the fleet mix, the power plus the hosting cost, the contracted revenue mix, and the DEPLOYMENT LAG"_ — with monitoring reframed away from raw busy-ness toward _"how many PROFITABLE TOKENS, the workloads, the model improvements are produced per GPU purchase. That is where the true capital efficiency sits,"_ on biweekly-to-monthly rolling forecasts.** On custom silicon (relevant to the [[GOOG]]/[[AMZN]] competitive leg, not to CRWV directly): _"Amazon… already looking at using their custom silicon for Trainium, Inferentia… GOOGLE IS ONLY LOOKING AT TPU. Microsoft Silicon helps ensure that they're not remaining dependent solely on third-party GPUs"_ — but framed as _"LESS ABOUT REPLACING GPUs OVERNIGHT"_ and as _"a strategic and financial lever… NOT AN IMMEDIATE REPLACEMENT for established GPU providers."_ ⚠️ **Weight the whole call as a three-month-stale practitioner framework, strong on MECHANISM and unreliable on TIMING.** (AlphaSense expert call, published 2026-07-22, interviewed 2026-04-23) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/GOOGL_-_ExpertCall_document_dated_05-08-2026.html) |

| 07-31 | **GS · Katherine Murphy / Michael Ng — AI Project Pulse (July 2026)** | capital | **mixed** | **★ The structural item, and it changes how the whole neocloud group should be underwritten: "Increasing adoption of GPU BACKSTOP PROGRAMS across neoclouds. Chipmakers like NVDA are providing its neocloud customers with a MINIMUM REVENUE GUARANTEE on underlying GPU capacity (in the event that the neocloud is unable to lease out its capacity to other customers) in exchange for A SHARE OF THE NEOCLOUD'S REVENUE ABOVE A CERTAIN LEVEL." NVDA named sovereign neoclouds FIRMUS and SHARON AI as early partners in the revenue-sharing and credit-support model.** ⚠️ **This is a collar written by the supplier on its own customers' demand risk: downside truncated, upside shared away. Two consequences worth carrying — (a) reported neocloud capacity and order flow are no longer clean reads on independent end-demand, since ordering can be sustained by the backstop; and (b) it advantages backstopped competitors relative to a CoreWeave that raises on its own credit, while concentrating the sector's credit chain on NVDA.** GS reads it positively for compute availability and for [[DELL]]/[[SMCI]]. **The same pattern is appearing beyond NVDA: Google is reportedly providing a CREDIT BACKSTOP plus CHIPS for an Anthropic 1.6GW / $15bn Texas campus** (WSJ, 2026-07-30). **Vendor-financed AI capacity is becoming a category, not a set of one-offs.** |

AICP deals at this time."* The stated purpose is the competitive point: get non-IG operators borrowing at IG pricing and build a track record until *"customer revenues and independent capital fund subsequent expansion."* ➜ **Read as a live variable for CRWV's relative cost of capital: the pause removes, at the margin, a subsidy that was accruing to sub-scale rivals.** 🔴 **③ THE REASSIGNMENT TEMPLATE, PRICED — WHAT A NEOCLOUD LEASE COSTS WHEN NVIDIA'S NAME IS ON IT: Hut 8 funded the first Beacon Point building with $4.25bn of senior secured notes at 6.129%, rated Baa2 and amortising from 2030, "which no Neocloud could price on its own name or for that term."*** The mechanic is credit substitution — the datacenter is financed against **NVDA as an AA-rated offtaker**, then the lease is handed to a neocloud (Lambda, in that deal) — and SemiAnalysis believes **NVDA likely stays primary obligor *"until the project significantly de-levers and/or clears a DSCR test"*** because *"lenders that bought Baa2 paper on an AA tenant will not consent to a B-rated one."* ⚠️ *Directional contrast only, different instruments: this page's own credit marks (MS leveraged-loan table, 08-20) quote Lambda's CORPORATE paper "LAMBCM" at 6.65%, 303→287bp — the project notes' Baa2 / 6.129% is precisely what the NVDA wrapper buys, and it is a cost of capital CRWV's competitors can now RENT rather than earn.* ⚠️ **UNIT HAZARD: every per-capacity figure in this note (AICP ~$58.6M/MW ≈ $59bn/GW, PORTS-Pike ~$24.7M/MW, the residual-value structure ~$9.4bn/GW) is MAXIMUM OBLIGATION / CREDIT-EXPOSURE INTENSITY — NOT revenue per watt and NOT build cost per GW. Never net them against, or chain them into a series with, either basis.** |

Both are margin arguments against the "GPU landlord" framing, and both are assertions with no numbers attached yet.** ✅ **INDEPENDENTLY CORROBORATED THE SAME WEEK, and this is the part that upgrades it from narrative to fact: Bloomberg/The Information report that [[MSFT]] *"has more recently been inking deals to RENT SPACE FROM NEOCLOUDS LIKE COREWEAVE, NSCALE AND [[NBIS]] NEBIUS"* as it manages a capacity shortage its own CFO says will persist ~12 months. The contracted number is nearly 3x the existing number — i.e. the exposure is in what CRWV has SIGNED, not what it currently runs, so it GROWS from here under the current book.** ➤ **Against that, the insulated comparables are stark: EQIX 95%, DLR 92% and CSQR 94% of existing US capacity in MAJOR METROS. Do not chain the three.** |

The argument: training is latency-insensitive and can live anywhere cheap, so CRWV's non-Tier-1 footprint is an ASSET while training scales; the moment the mix shifts from training toward inference (which is latency-sensitive and must sit near population centres), that same footprint becomes a LIABILITY it cannot quickly re-site.** ⚠️ **Note the direct tension with the [[NVDA]]/Bernstein read published in the SAME weekly — Stacy Rasgon arguing AI demand is *"largely driven by INFERENCE workloads as agentic AI continues to gain adoption."* If Rasgon is already right about the mix, Madison's adverse scenario is not a hypothetical about slowing training; it is a description of the present. The two Bernstein analysts are not reconciled in the note and are NOT reconciled here.** ⚠️ Spec-sales relay of a research note; the underlying note's rating and PT are not given in the email. |

The −40% is a 20% COST OF EQUITY plus a framework that assigns NO EXIT VALUE at all.** 🔴🔴 **THE DDTL LADDER, IN FULL: SOFR+962bp (Jul-23) → +425bp (Sep-24) → +400bp (OpenAI, Jul-25) → +225bp (Meta, Mar-26, ≈5.9% all-in) → +450bp (two non-IG, May-26) → +550bp (undisclosed, Aug-26, $2.6bn, matures Sep-2031, ≈9% all-in), against customer contracts averaging ~3 years.** ✅ **AND THE UNIT FIX: $45bn/GW is REVENUE per GW per YEAR (the short-term cohort, 4.5x the ~$10bn/GW long-term cohort) = $45m/MW/yr — directly comparable with CRWV's own ~$40M/MW short-dated marks, not a separate basis.** |

**Quarter synthesis:** the debate moved off demand (consensus: sold out, Cantor sees an RPO beat + Anthropic) to the *quality* of earnings and the capital structure — Bernstein attacks the AOI guide as unhittable and fixed debt as a disadvantage vs hyperscalers, while NVDA doubles down (+$2B) and the Vera Rubin ramp becomes the new execution risk. Layered on top, Barron's mainstream-press read (05-18, 06-01) adds a recurring-insider-selling data point — 349,000 shares / $44.372M the week of 05-11/15, then ~$1.9M more from two board members on 06-01 as the stock "jumped 45%" — alongside its own restatement of the hard numbers (record $740M net loss, $99.4B backlog/$21B Meta deal, $31–35B debt-funded capex). None of it is thesis-changing on its own, but the pattern across two consecutive months is worth tracking alongside the widening loss. **New into July:** Meta's reported "Meta Compute" pivot (Bloomberg, via Barclays 2026-07-01) reframes the largest known single-customer relationship — Citi sizes ~1/3 of backlog as Meta-tied and reads the pivot as making further Meta-CRWV deals unlikely, a fresh bear signal for neoclouds broadly; Barclays' own stance stays middle-of-the-road (EW, PT $120) rather than turning bearish outright. Watch whether this is corroborated by other desks or fades as speculative Bloomberg-sourced reporting. **Into the print (07-09):** Bernstein's earnings preview (still UP/$67) crystallizes the setup — demand is not the question; the binding constraint per IR is **financing, not demand** (a point the bull side now shares), and the quality-of-earnings test is a **2H AOI bogey of $790M-1.05B** plus whether the "timing-based, not economic" margin framing is validated by the **1.7GW year-end ramp** and an upward AOI trajectory. 2026 revenue is expected within the guided range. **Mid-July tape (07-13→15):** the capital-markets read stays constructive — Morgan Stanley's **$3.1B CRWV loan drew >$19B in orders (~6x)**, deep AI-infra debt appetite (bull for financeability) though @theinformation flags the loans "may outlast the chips/contracts" (the same duration-mismatch bear); and CRWV is now reportedly **exploring derivatives to hedge memory & storage chip prices** (Reuters) — a novel treasury move that reads as prudent risk-management into a possible memory-cycle turn but implicitly concedes forward component-cost exposure. On the Meta debate, FundaAI's Weekly corroborates the SemiAnalysis bull counter — Meta's next-year capex is running "shockingly high" (some FY27 ests ~$220bn) with CoreWeave named among Meta's recent major compute deals, cutting against the Meta-capex-cut fear that drove the early-July neocloud sell-off. **07-24 fold (SemiAnalysis 07-06 full-note credit detail, previously unlogged):** the financing debate now has hard marks on both sides. Bear side — CRWV **5yr unsecured at ~10%** and the arithmetic that moving all-in funding from **5.62% to 10% collapses PBT margin from 14.8% to 5.4%** at 70-80% LTV, plus **neocloud colo leases at a 3-5% higher yield-on-cost** than hyperscale/IG deals; bull side — with a hyperscaler offtake attached, CRWV funds at **5.9% (Meta-backstopped DDTL 4.0 fixed tranche), only ~90bps wide of Meta's own ~5.0% 5yr bond**, i.e. the market prices CRWV project-execution risk at 90bps, not at a distress premium, and NVDA's new backstop is explicitly designed to let CRWV fund shorter-tenor/diversified books somewhere between those two marks. Read together with the 07-16 BofA CDS datapoint (well off the ~800bps late-2025 peak) and the 6x-oversubscribed MS loan, the credit-market evidence has moved decisively toward "financeable at a spread that depends on who the offtaker is" — which reframes the Meta-concentration debate as a **funding-cost** question, not only a demand question.

**Quarter synthesis — UPDATED 2026-08-25 (3 sources: SemiAnalysis · Dylan Patel podcast 08-25; Citi “Inference Ahead — Investing in Open Models” 08-21; Tier-2 backfill Damnang / “The Optical Edge” 05-05. NOTHING SUPERSEDED — no CRWV rating, PT, backlog, capex or margin figure on this page was overwritten; none of the three carries a CRWV rating or PT).** The debate above stands; what this window adds is the OUTSIDE view of the business model, and it is uncomfortable in a specific, useful way. **Dylan Patel describes CoreWeave’s structure without naming it — “you go to a random cloud… I need to turn around and find a customer… then I take the customer’s commitment to the credit markets and I raise the capital” — against balance-sheet builders who “build compute WITHOUT FINDING A CUSTOMER” and therefore “control what’s going on.” That is the option-value asymmetry stated plainly: in a rising price market, the contract-first builder sold its upside at signing.** **His price call follows from the same mechanism rather than from a demand view: “most compute will still continue to transact at SUB $20 BILLION A GIGAWATT” even at end-2027, “because ALL OF IT HAS TO BE FINANCED.” ⚠️⚠️ Revenue per GW per year, not build cost — not to be netted against this page’s capex or debt-per-MW marks.** **Citi supplies the tape that cuts the other way: Blackwell-class spot at $5.29/hour, up 30% YTD and up ~14% since its own June edition, which Citi attributes to “infrastructure constraints on incremental supply” — but then draws the conclusion that this favours the HYPERSCALERS, not the neoclouds. ⚠️ It is an index rate, not CoreWeave’s realised contract pricing, and the great majority of revenue does not reprice with it.** **The Tier-2 backfill is worth having as a dated fossil of the bear case at its most compact — “these businesses don’t own physical bottlenecks; THEY LEASE THEM,” with Microsoft at 62-72% of 2025 revenue and the Chanos ~0% return-on-capital line — precisely because two of its three supports have since moved (customer concentration has broadened, the financing stack is far larger) while the third, the ROC argument, has not been retired by anything on this page.**

**Quarter synthesis — ADDENDUM 2026-09-02 (1 source: Jefferies' 58pp "AI Deep Dive" chart deck, Brent Thill). A NEW HOUSE ARRIVES ON THIS PAGE AT THE TOP OF THE PUBLISHED RANGE, AND NOTHING IS SUPERSEDED.** **Jefferies initiates its presence here with BUY / PT $150 — no prior Jefferies rating or PT existed (desk colour only), so this is additive, and it ties the highest published target on the page alongside DB's Buy $150 (08-12) against JPM Neutral $120, Barclays EW $105 and Bernstein Underperform $67.** The synthesis above framed the open question as the *quality* of earnings and the capital stack rather than demand. This source pushes on the first half in three ways and leaves the second untouched. **(1) It puts the first dated, quantified EBIT-margin destination on the page — "margins move towards mid 20s by 2028" — which is the specific claim the Bernstein/Barclays execution bear now has to be argued against rather than the vaguer "EBIT ramp".** **(2) It upgrades the provenance of the single best margin datapoint of the quarter: the "5 to 10 percentage points" contribution-margin step-up is now sourced to Intrator on the call rather than to JPM's read of it.** **(3) It supplies the cost-side rebuttal the memory-inflation bear was missing — the CFO's "we’re also passing through component price increases," set inside Jefferies' evidence that hyperscaler cloud operating margins expanded for four straight quarters to 38.9% in 2Q26.** ⚠️ **What it does NOT do, and the reason this stays a quality-of-earnings quarter rather than a resolved one: the 7x is struck on ROLLED-FORWARD CY28 EV/EBIT. The valuation case is an out-year one on the very estimate the bears dispute, so it argues the multiple is cheap without adding evidence that the CY28 EBIT is right. The capital-stack leg — ~10x-revenue capex intensity, the debt cost and the MS-Credit-priced 8.2-14.2% offtake paper — is not addressed by this source at all and stands exactly as logged.**

**Quarter synthesis — ADDENDUM 2026-09-09 (1 source: the Bloomberg LIVE transcript of Mike Intrator, CEO, at the GS Communacopia + Technology Conference, 2026-09-08 — a MANAGEMENT source, not a broker note). NOTHING SUPERSEDED, AND ONE MANAGEMENT CLAIM NOW SITS DIRECTLY AGAINST A PRICED DATAPOINT ALREADY ON THIS PAGE.** The Q3'26 window's two live questions are whether operating leverage keeps compounding and what the debt costs. This transcript answers neither with a number — **no backlog, capex, power, pricing or margin figure was given at all** — but it does three things. **First, it answers the inference-migration bear structurally rather than numerically:** CoreWeave says it does not run a training fleet and an inference fleet, only "AI infrastructure", which if true removes the mechanism by which cheaper inference silicon strands its assets — and which nothing in-corpus can verify, because no workload split is disclosed. **Second, it puts the CEO's cost-of-capital claim ("at par or close to at par with many of the hyperscalers") against the MS Global Credit Strategy tape of 08-20 (300–600bp offtake gap; CRWV BB term loan 9.15%) — now the cleanest live disagreement on this page between management's story and observable prices.** **Third, it adds an asset-LIGHT line to an asset-heavy story:** NVIDIA reference-architecture designation plus software licensed onto third-party balance sheets, one deal closed and more claimed in the pipe, with no revenue attached. ⚠️ **Transcript status: LIVE with heavy proper-noun garbling — re-read the FINAL when it lands.**

**Quarter synthesis — ADDENDUM 2026-09-14 (1 source: the analyst's own GS Communacopia notes on the SAME Intrator session already folded on 09-09; MANAGEMENT, no rating, PT or estimate). THE SYNTHESIS AND THE `Signal vs management` TABLE BOTH STAND — the session was already scored as strategically constructive but not estimate-changing, and that holds. ONE STANDING CLAIM FINALLY GAINS A METRIC: the moving-up-the-stack argument, carried qualitatively on this page since May, now has a number — approximately 75% of customers use at least THREE CoreWeave services, with management saying that understates potential because many AI-natives are too early in their lifecycle for a second or third. ⚠ Company-reported, denominator undefined, BREADTH NOT REVENUE, and NOT nettable against the top-customer concentration figure — but it is the first falsifiable marker for whether diversification arrives before the anchor contracts roll off, which is the entire bull case. TWO POSTURE ITEMS ADDED, NEITHER ESTIMATE-CHANGING: management expects INDUSTRY CONSOLIDATION on execution and financing (an incumbent arguing capital access is the filter, from a borrower whose offtake paper still prices 300-600bp wide of Amazon's), and MODEL NEUTRALITY stated explicitly against [[AVGO]]'s closed-model preference — a claim about DEMAND COMPOSITION, not about revenue per token, and the second leg was not addressed.**

**🆕 Quarter synthesis — ADDENDUM 2026-09-21 (/run-inbox 23h; 1 source: the FULL 148-page Rothschild & Co Redburn PRIMARY, which now replaces the 21h spec-sales relay as the authority on this note). THE RATING AND THE TARGET ARE UNCHANGED AT SELL / $54 AND NOTHING ON THIS PAGE WAS SUPERSEDED — BUT THE *REASON* IS NOW VISIBLE, AND IT IS NOT THE ONE THE RELAY IMPLIED.** The relay read as a demand-and-pricing bear: highest-conviction negative view, *"current valuations assume stronger demand, utilisation and pricing than the fundamentals support"*. The primary's own model says something much narrower and far more useful: **Redburn's CRWV revenue sits 0.7-4.1% BELOW VA consensus and its 2027/28 operating income 4.5-4.6% below — essentially in line.** The −40% therefore comes almost entirely from two now-disclosed method choices: a **20% cost of equity** at the name (against 14% in the illustrative sector model) and a framework that **assigns no exit value whatsoever**, explicitly set against *"consensus price targets, which often include more optimistic exit or terminal value assumptions."* ➤ **That reframes the quarter's open question. It is no longer "is the backlog real" or "is pricing holding" — Redburn concedes both in its own numbers. It is whether CoreWeave's equity deserves a 14% or a 20% discount rate, which is a question about the FINANCING STACK, and that is exactly where the note's hardest evidence sits: a DDTL ladder running from SOFR+225bp on Meta-backed paper to SOFR+550bp on the newest undisclosed-counterparty facility, and modelled borrowings rising to $110.7bn by 2029 against $8.9bn of shareholders' equity.** ✅ **AND ONE CROSS-PAGE UNIT PROBLEM IS CLOSED: $45bn/GW is REVENUE per GW per year (Fig 32), i.e. $45m/MW/yr — which lines up with CoreWeave's own ~$40M/MW short-dated marks and with [[NBIS]]'s $40-50M/MW. Those three are the SAME measurement on the same basis, not three incompatible ones. The 21h relay block's guard (a), which called it a "compute price", is left in place as the relay-era record and is corrected in the PRIMARY FOLD above.**

## Changelog
- **🆕 2026-09-22 (/run-inbox, work unit D) — 1 source: UBS Securities LLC · Karl Keirstead / Chris Giuditta, with UBS Evidence Lab, *"CoreWeave Inc — Initiation of Coverage: Launch With a Buy, Strong Growth Offsets Credit Concerns"*, 2026-09-22, 58pp.** Added 1 dated block in `## Current state`, 1 intra-quarter full-log row (09-22), 1 new `Sinal vs gestão` row (**Enterprise breadth — the diversification leg**), an extension to the **Q3'26 synthesis**, 1 Sources bullet and this record. ⚠️ **NOTHING SUPERSEDED IN THE BODY. No rating, price target, estimate, guide or GW mark already on this page was overwritten — UBS's own cover states *"Prior: No Rating"*, so this is a net-new house rather than a revision, and the existing PT set (Redburn Sell $54 · Bernstein UP $67 · JPM Neutral $120 · Barclays EW $105 · DB Buy $150 · Arete Buy $317) is untouched.** 🔴 **ONE OPEN ITEM ON THIS PAGE IS NOW ANSWERED AND THE OLD TEXT IS DELIBERATELY LEFT IN PLACE RATHER THAN REWRITTEN:** the 09-17 Barclays block carries an explicit instruction not to net ~$40mn/MW against the ~$16mn/MW/yr base *"until the contract TERM behind the $40mn is known"*, because *"the note does NOT state whether $40mn/MW is TOTAL CONTRACT VALUE or ANNUALISED."* UBS now states both — **3-6 month contracts, quoted as "up to $40 million per MW ($40 billion per GW)", i.e. ANNUALISED, and "3-4x the monetization per MW of the installed base."** The Barclays warning stands as written (it was an accurate description of the Barclays note); what changes is that the missing term and basis are now on the record from a third house, and the two figures are confirmed as **different contract TIERS rather than the same price at two dates**. 🔭 **PRICING FORK — STILL OPEN, NOW FOUR-WAY BUT ONLY THREE-WAY IN SUBSTANCE:** UBS's ~$11bn/GW is a **blended realised rate on the INSTALLED BASE**, not a forward contract price, and is therefore compatible with both the CoreWeave-management FLOOR (09-21) and the Nscale-S-1 CEILING (09-18). - **2026-08-04 (/run-inbox) — READ-THROUGH from Bernstein QUICK TAKE, Global Digital Assets "AI Infra/miners: Texas data center audit to drive more MW scarcity" (Gautam Chhugani / Mahika Sapra / Sanskar Chindalia / Harsh Misra, 2026-08-04, slug `Bernstein_WULF_AI_Inframiners_Texas_data_center_audit_to_drive_more_M.html`). Added 1 Risks sub-bullet (under "Supply / NVIDIA dependence") + 1 intra-quarter Full-log row (08-04, supply/mixed) + 1 Sources entry. ⚠️ NO RATING, PT OR ESTIMATE ON CRWV CHANGED — Bernstein carries no CRWV rating in this note (its standing CRWV call remains Underperform / PT $67 from Madison Rezaei, unchanged and untouched here); nothing on this page superseded.** The note's subject is the **Texas ERCOT audit and the bitcoin-miner / AI-infra cohort**, not CoreWeave. **Two items folded: ① the one CRWV-specific fact, and it cuts in CRWV's favour — Abbott has directed the PUCT and ERCOT to audit ALL data center projects in ERCOT's interconnection process and ERCOT has PAUSED the "Batch Zero" review that was due to publish classification results on 2026-08-07, but "CORZ has already delivered 437 MW to Coreweave… and remains on track to scale up to 590MW by early 2027. Thus, CORZ execution timelines are not impacted" — i.e. CoreWeave's contracted Core Scientific capacity is INSULATED from the Batch Zero delay, with only CORZ's 300 MW gross Pecos expansion exposed. That is a partial de-risking of the "datacenter-provider delivery" leg of the supply risk already on this page, and it matters against the 1.7 GW year-end ramp Bernstein's own preview (07-09) is holding IR to. ② The offsetting read, which is the more consequential one: the note's whole thesis is that regulatory throttling of new interconnect capacity makes ALREADY-APPROVED MW MORE VALUABLE — "if MWs are scarce, it makes sense to earn more per MW" — evidenced by IREN's revenue yield per IT MW rising 50%, from ~$10mn under its Microsoft contract to ~$15mn under its NVIDIA contract, plus enterprise cloud deals at GPU-hour rates 20-25% ABOVE previous guidance. CoreWeave leases every one of its datacenters ($10.1B operating-lease ROU), so a rising $/MW for the host/landlord cohort is a rising COST of capacity sourcing — compounding the 3-5% higher yield-on-cost SemiAnalysis already measures on neocloud colo leases (07-06) — even as it validates pricing power on the sell side (consistent with Baker's contracted-base-repricing-higher argument, 08-04).** ⚠️ **Basis discipline recorded with it: the source's $/IT MW and $/gross MW yield series are separate and must not be mixed.** **Routing note: CORZ, IREN, CIFR, WULF, RIOT, CLSK and MARA have NO wiki pages, so all are written as plain-text tickers here — no wikilinks. Full cohort tables, the ERCOT 474 GW queue figure (interconnection-QUEUE basis) and the logged RIOT ~1 GW-vs-1.7 GW internal inconsistency live on [themes/ai-datacenter-power](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md), which is this source's primary home.** (CORZ covered by Madison Rezaei.)
- **2026-07-31 (wiki-ingest) — a new listed competitor coming at a valuation and revenue claim that would exceed CoreWeave's, plus a supplier-written demand collar that changes how the group should be underwritten.** No rating or PT superseded. **① Nscale (UK, NVDA-backed) is preparing an IPO with GS and JPM — investor day in New York the week of Aug 3, listing possibly this fall — at a floated ~$25bn vs $14.6bn in March. ⚠️ The claim that matters: Nscale projects 2027 revenue TRIPLING TO $30BN, "more than what analysts estimate CoreWeave will generate next year." The visible weakness in that number: its West Virginia flagship still needs "tens of billions" of financing AND HAS NO ANNOUNCED TENANT.** The playbook is explicitly CoreWeave's — **Nscale is buying Anyscale for $1.6bn, likened directly to CRWV's $1.7bn Weights & Biases purchase just before its March 2025 IPO.** Backers: >$6bn from 8090 Industries, Citadel, Jane Street; **Sheryl Sandberg and Nick Clegg** added to the board. ⚠️ Tape context against the timing: **CRWV −25% over the past month; Sharon AI −42% over the past month after +60% since its February IPO; Lambda delayed its listing; Crusoe talking to bankers** (The Information · Valida Pau, 2026-07-30). **② The structural item, and the most consequential for how this page reads competitor capacity: NVDA is writing GPU BACKSTOP PROGRAMS — a MINIMUM REVENUE GUARANTEE on GPU capacity a neocloud cannot lease out, in exchange for a share of revenue above a threshold, with FIRMUS and SHARON AI named as early partners** (GS · Murphy/Ng, AI Project Pulse July 2026, 2026-07-31). ⚠️ **Two consequences: (a) reported neocloud capacity and order flow are no longer clean reads on independent end-demand, because ordering can be sustained by the backstop rather than by lettings; and (b) it advantages backstopped competitors against a CoreWeave raising on its own credit, while concentrating the sector's credit chain on a single counterparty. The same pattern is appearing beyond NVDA — Google is reportedly providing a credit backstop PLUS CHIPS for an Anthropic 1.6GW / $15bn Texas campus (WSJ, 2026-07-30). Vendor-financed AI capacity is now a category, not a set of one-offs.** **③ CRWV was among the largest holdings in the force-sold Situational Awareness book (+22% on the unwind day, vs NBIS +27%, BE +27%, SNDK +26%). Fund detail: ~$20bn AUM, +439% in 1H26, up to 4x levered, then −67% in July; ~$16bn public book sold to Citadel in one block (MLP and Jane Street also bid); ~$5bn Anthropic stake retained.** ⚠️ Reading both ways: July's drawdown was substantially forced selling, **but CRWV −25% over the month shows the damage preceded and exceeded the bounce.** Cross-filed [[NBIS]], [[NVDA]], [[DELL]], [[SMCI]], [[ANTHROPIC]], [[GOOG]], [themes/ai-datacenter-power](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md).

- **2026-07-27** — **/wiki-ingest (Twitter corpus backfill). 2 intra-quarter rows. Nothing superseded.** Corpus material from 07-16/19 that sat past this page's high-water mark. **(1) The put-options story, with a contrarian reading worth recording: @jukan05 — 'I don't understand why the news that CoreWeave is CONSIDERING PUT OPTIONS is being interpreted as bearish. Isn't it actually bullish, since it implies that THE LTAs WOULD REMAIN IN PLACE EVEN IF MEMORY PRICES COLLAPSE?' The logic is that hedging memory-price downside is only rational when the underlying long-term agreements are firm — making the hedge evidence of contract durability rather than distress. Filed with the limitation stated: this is a market interpretation, CoreWeave has not confirmed the structure, and no size or counterparty is reported.** **(2) The more useful datapoint, because it is measurable and cuts against the week's dominant narrative: @deedydas documented Kimi K3 hitting #10 ON OPENROUTER AT ~140B TOKENS/DAY WITHIN TWO DAYS OF RELEASE, WITH SERVING INFRASTRUCTURE DEGRADING — THROUGHPUT DOWN FROM 30 TOK/S TO 13 TOK/S AND END-TO-END LATENCY UP TO ~72s.** The market read K3 as a deflationary efficiency shock to compute and memory demand ('the DeepSeek moment' framing that drove Friday's selloff across [[MU]]/[[SNDK]]/[[NBIS]]); the observable first-order effect was **serving capacity failing to keep up with adoption** — which is incremental inference demand for exactly the capacity neoclouds sell. Consistent with FUNDA's independent argument the same week that K3 raises accelerator, HBM and scale-up-networking intensity rather than lowering it. **Neither row changes the standing debate on offtake concentration, which the tape re-priced on Friday ([[NBIS]] -15% on an '[[ANTHROPIC]] air pocket' narrative) — they bear on contract durability and demand, not on customer diversification.** Cross-filed [[NBIS]], [[MU]], [[ANTHROPIC]].
